**Canonical repo path:** `docs/specs/attorney-review-queue.md` — filed 2026-07-26. Duplicate-routing check run at filing: no equivalent review-queue document existed; the only prior partial is `trcp-deadline-skeleton-2026-03-01.md` §9 (TRCP entries only), which now points here and remains the TRCP-priority detail.

**Why this exists.** The mining has outrun the ruling. Research has produced propositions faster than they can be verified, and the queue — not the research — is now the project's bottleneck (H22). This document is the whole queue in one place, so the bottleneck is at least visible.

**Convention:** ✅ = closed (2026-07-26 unless a later date is stated) · ⬜ = open. Each open item carries **the actual question in bold**.

**Scope — what this file does NOT hold (ruled 2026-08-19).** The GO-LIVE GATES are not in this queue, and they are not missing from it. Gates 1–10, `GL-1`'s floor items, and the trigger-1 instructions revision (`T#1`) live in `docs/specs/Go_Live_Gates.md`, which is canonical and binding for them; their STATUS lives in `BUILD-STATE.md`, which is the only authority on it. **They await Michael's ACTION, not his RULING** — and that is the line this register is drawn on. - ✅ **In re Allstate Indem. Co., 622 S.W.3d 870 (Tex. 2021)** — read 2026-07-26. Docket No. 20-0071 confirmed; two-*Allstate* question closed. §18.001(f) qualification holding; reasonable-notice content standard; reasonable notice ≠ Rule 702/*Robinson* reliability.

- ✅ **In re Auburn Creek Ltd. P'ship, 655 S.W.3d 837 (Tex. 2022)** — read 2026-07-26. Nexus element has independent content; state it separately.
- ✅ **In re H.E.B. Grocery Co., 492 S.W.3d 300 (Tex. 2016)** — read 2026-07-26. Less-intrusive-means analysis captured.

- ✅ **Tex. Est. Code ch. 352** — **full chapter read 2026-07-26.** Both queue questions answered: "on proof satisfactory to the court" **IS** the operative standard (the 2026-07-25 voice note was right), and the two-lane separation survives **more strongly than framed** — the lanes are separate **subchapters** (A compensation/commission; B expenses and attorney's fees), an architectural feature, not a practice caution. **But the profile classification FAILED:** `discretionary-equitable` is wrong on both halves — §352.051 says the representative **"is entitled to"** (entitlement, not court permission), and **"equitable and just" appears nowhere in ch. 352**. Second consecutive session in which a voice-session classification failed against primary text (cf. O1). **§352.052 was missing from our docs entirely** — will-contest allowance, with the defend-vs-contest asymmetry. Corrected classification and full treatment: `statutes-pass-est352-cprc71-2026-07-26.md`.
  - ⬜ **Residual (V14a): what procedural vehicle carries a §352.051(2) fee request?** V14 ruled that fees sit outside §352.053, and §352.053 is the only procedure ch. 352 states — so the chapter is silent on the vehicle, and the vehicle is what the export must be shaped against.
  - ⬜ **Residual (V16, narrowed): does "is entitled to" bind the court**, or is it looser than "shall award"? The schema half dissolved into the O6 decomposition; this legal half stands.
  - ⬜ **Residual (V17): are a §71.004(c)-compelled representative's fees reimbursable under §352.051(2)** where the WD recovery is not the estate's? **Decides whether the probate fee profile touches PI at all.**
- ✅ **Tex. Civ. Prac. & Rem. Code ch. 71** — **full chapter read 2026-07-26.** **WD proceeds are not estate funds, on three independent hooks:** §71.004(a) exclusive benefit of surviving spouse, children, parents; §71.010(b) apportionment in the jury's verdict among those alive at that time; §71.011 not subject to the decedent's debts. §352.002's commission base therefore has nothing to attach to on a WD-only file — **including** the §71.004(c) case where the representative is compelled to prosecute. **WD half of V15 closed against the commission, pending sign-off.**
  - ⬜ **Residual (V15, survival half): OPEN and load-bearing.** **CLAUDE CORRECTION, recorded:** Claude stated in-session that a survival recovery is estate property. **§71.021(b) does not say that** — the action survives "to and in favor of the **heirs, legal representatives, and estate**," three unranked takers. Whether funds pass through the representative in administration cannot be closed from ch. 71; needs case law, same shape as V4. Even where they flow, §352.002(b)(2)(C) excludes cash paid to an heir or legatee as such, so the commission would compute on the receipt side only.

### Criminal-side requirements (CR series) — ADDED 2026-08-12

*(**Series CREATED by Michael's ruling 2026-08-12**, the WF-1/DE precedent: criminal practice items fit neither FE nor IN nor DE. **The #48 elicitation caveat applies to CR as it does to IN and DE — renaming is a one-word veto until these IDs get load-bearing.** **COLLISION FLAGGED, DELIBERATELY NOT RENAMED (the H-series disposition):** `CR-3` already means the **Texas crash-report form** in this file at IN-2 and in `contact-directory.md` §7. That is a form designation, not an ID series, so the packet's collision test did not fire — but the string now carries two meanings in this file. Michael was asked before these rows landed and chose to proceed as CR with the clash flagged; renaming an attorney's ID series is not Code's call. **When citing one, say "CR-3 (criminal requirement)" or "the CR-3 crash report."**)*

- ⬜ **CR-1 — Hearing-date extraction with attorney confirmation at matter creation.** From the appointment paperwork: when a matter is created from an OAA, prompt as to whether a hearing is already set — it may be in the paperwork — and if so pull it from the document. Priority: now. **Dedupe instruction carried:** check against `criminal-appointment-intake-and-docket-enhancements.md` at HEAD; if the intake spec already covers it, this records as a second observation on the covering section rather than a standalone item.
- ⬜ **CR-2 — Per-cause prosecutor assignment, by-prosecutor grouping of the day's docket, and a county prosecutor roster with collision-safe short codes.** Prosecutors ask off-hand "what cases do we have together today?" and the answer must be readable from page 1. **Question riding here (§5 Q1): should the prosecutor assignment live at the CAUSE level or at the DOCKET-ENTRY level**, given that one defendant's multiple causes were split between two prosecutors on the same docket, and that the assignment observed at court differed from the printed docket on roughly three of eight defendants? Priority: now. The roster half is **CD-1 directory substrate** (contacts with roles) — annotated onto the CD entries too.
- ⬜ **CR-3 — At-court correction capture** (prosecutor, attorney-of-record, setting type) that persists to the matter and reconciles against the next published docket, with a **court-confirmed flag distinguishing observed fact from imported data**. Treat printed docket data as unreliable: live evidence includes a blank attorney field resolving on the next docket and a clerk attorney-code error flagged in the docket's own error log. Cross-links CR-2. Priority: soon. *(This is CR-3 the criminal requirement — not the CR-3 crash-report form cited at IN-2.)*
- ⬜ **CR-4 — Front-page expected-disposition marker** (plea / contest / status) **plus per-setting appearance outcome** (appeared, no-show, in custody elsewhere) feeding the follow-up list and the next worksheet's banners. Live evidence: plea-expected cases marked by hand with a "P"; no-shows recorded in the margins; one no-show client on bond; a matter needing a reset after a bench-out did not happen. Priority: soon.
- ⬜ **CR-5 — Custody status per client** — facility, county/state, and the **source and date of the information** — **including out-of-state facilities**, plus **bench-warrant/writ status**, both surfaced in the client-page banner. Live evidence: a revocation client confined in another state's correctional facility, with handwritten notes tracking that he did not get benched out here and needed a reset. Priority: soon.
- ⬜ **CR-6 — Designated scratch and quick-intake blocks on the worksheet, plus a post-court reconciliation step** that routes captured notes to the correct matters and creates new matters from intake blocks. Live evidence of what happens without them: one client's negotiation numbers written on a different client's page; a new case for an existing client written upside-down on a blank back; a full new-client intake handwritten on the front page. **Question riding here (§5 Q7): when handwritten court notes are transcribed and routed to matters after court, what marks the resulting record as ATTORNEY-REVIEWED rather than raw OCR, so downstream worksheets don't print unverified transcription as fact?** Priority: soon.
- ⬜ **CR-7 — Three-way number mapping: defense cause number ↔ docket entry number ↔ DA file/incident number.** Stored per cause, printed on the worksheet, optionally imported from the DA's charge-table document. Live evidence: the DA's own charge-table printout hand-annotated — indicted rows circled, numbered, and mapped to docket entries — because the worksheet carries only cause and docket numbers while the DA's office talks in its own file numbers. **Question riding here (§5 Q5): manual entry at court, OCR-assisted import of the DA charge table, or both — and can the mapping print as a front-page column without crowding the handwriting space?** Priority: now.
- ⬜ **CR-8 — Offer lifecycle tracking: offer versions as first-class records** (date, source, full terms, option sets), with the worksheet rendering history, a changed/unchanged flag, and option sets. Live evidence: one offer tracked through three versions with dates and sources (email vs. offer sheet vs. revised sheet); another flagged "UNCHANGED from the sheet you had before the last setting"; a two-option offer laid out as a client choice; a probation option disappearing and returning. **The email-workflow doc's criminal plea-offer observation is its unruled relative — cross-referenced, never merged.** Priority: now.
- ⬜ **CR-9 — Notes attach to the MATTER, not the worksheet, and print forward automatically** — including transcribed handwritten notes from CR-6's reconciliation step. Live evidence: the later worksheet reproduced the earlier one's typed notes in CARRY-OVER blocks so nothing was lost, but handwritten content did not carry and had to be re-copied by hand; a phone number was handwritten twice and never entered any system. Absorbs the print-forward half of the contacts-at-capture requirement. Priority: now.
- ⬜ **CR-10 — Plea-paperwork audit rendered as a signing checklist:** degree of offense vs. charged statute, jail-credit presence for in-custody/bench-warrant defendants, completeness of required certifications, and non-concurrent cost totals. **The capture's own highest-value item.** Live evidence, all caught on paper before the judge signed: zero days' jail credit printed for a defendant who had been sitting on a bench warrant; the degree of offense misprinted on two causes; a blank, unsigned certification of right to appeal on every cause; and court costs that do not run concurrent. **HARD GATE, restated from CLAUDE.md rule 1: no computed check runs while the underlying propositions are UNVERIFIED.** Those six propositions now live in `docs/specs/legal-rule-registry-criminal-plea-and-costs.md` (all UNVERIFIED), and **CR-10 is gated on their verification.** **Question riding here (§5 Q6): can these checks be encoded as rules run against drafted judgments, and which verified-authority registry do they check against? — ANSWERED IN PART by the ruling: that registry file, once verified.** Priority: soon, but highest-value.
- ⬜ **CR-11 — Rule-generated banners from matter state** (prior FTA with a transportation concern, bench-warrant history, clerk data error, new appointment, changed offer) **plus a "FOLLOW-UPS — not on tomorrow's docket" closing page** fed by open tasks, **plus quick task capture from court notes** (a records subpoena to issue, mitigation to gather, items to run down with the court coordinator). Priority: soon.
- ⬜ **CR-CONSTRAINT (no ID — BINDING DESIGN CONSTRAINT, not a feature): all worksheet semantics must survive BLACK-AND-WHITE printing.** Borders, shading density, weight, and icons — **never color-coded meaning.** Michael prints in black and white only; the highlighting observed on one worksheet was a one-time conversation aid and was expressly **not** a feature request. **This governs ALL worksheet rendering, including every CR item above.** **Question riding here (§5 Q4): which black-and-white-safe typographic conventions should carry banner and flag semantics?** FE-10's format profiles are the civil-side sibling — **cross-referenced, never merged.**
- ⬜ **`spec-feedback.md`, 2026-07-25 OAA entry** — **do you want a cause-number-pending reminder, and should gender and race be captured at all?** **WIDENED 2026-08-12 (#64), NOT resolved: the CR-3 intake path raises the identical question.** The crash report carries ethnicity and sex fields, so if extraction is ever built it faces the same choice the OAA path already put to you. **One answer should govern both intake paths** — recorded here so they cannot drift into two different defaults. **RULED 2026-08-13 (#66), BOTH HALVES: CAPTURE BOTH sex and race/ethnicity, on both intake paths (OAA + CR-3), one answer governing both — #64's cross-reference is satisfied. Cause-number-pending reminder: YES — a visible flag on matters created without a cause number, cleared on entry.** Annotated add-only on the spec-feedback entry the same batch; the question CLOSES. *(Ruling is DESIGN status — nothing is authorized to be built from it; no field exists on the party registry today.)*

### Form engine (FE series) — ADDED 2026-08-07

**This series was absent from this file entirely** until the 2026-08-07 rulings batch; it had lived only in `form-engine.md` §12.6. Added here so the register is complete. `form-engine.md` §12.6 carries the finding text and the ruling detail.

- ✅ **FE-1 — chronology sources carry provider identities but NOT addresses, phones, or billed charges; the engine cannot ship a servable draft from a chronology alone. Where does provider data come from — §4 interview cards, a persistent provider-directory table, or another source?** **CLOSED 2026-08-07: the persistent provider-directory table**, with the §4 interview card surviving as the fallback for a provider not yet in the directory, writing back into it (enter-once). Three sub-rulings, all confirmed: **(a) identity in the directory, dollars on the case** — billed charges are per-case facts, and storing them per-provider would recreate the wrong-level defect class CL-2 was built to kill; **(b) Option A pointer model** — case-level provider records point at the directory entry and read live, with the served .docx files as the historical record of what went out; **(c) silent trust** — directory fills carry no confirm flags, since the data is attorney-entered and the proofread every filing gets already catches staleness. Michael's own load-bearing requirement, recorded: editing a provider **from within a case** must propagate to every linked case, which only the pointer model delivers. Build-facing consequences for whenever the engine is authorized: the directory entry must be editable in case context, and that edit surface must state firm-wide scope with a linked-case count (labeling, not a confirm click). **Spec ruling only — authorizes no table, migration, or UI.** **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-08 by CD-1** (contact-directory reframe): the ruling stands for the provider question as framed; the live question is CD-1. Surviving mechanics carry into CD-1 as PROPOSED. See `rulings-capture-2026-08-08.md` Parts 1–2. **Evidence channel opened 2026-08-10:** the `drafting-disclosures` skill (`docs/skills/drafting-disclosures/SKILL.md`) emits a mandatory provider-data block every run — designed as the FE-1 scout, to accumulate a real provider corpus so the directory-vs-interview-cards question could be ruled on evidence. Since FE-1 closed and was superseded, **that evidence now feeds CD-1**, not FE-1. Blocks are saved by Michael's hand outside the repo; the skill never writes them here.

Because document names are messy (abbreviations, records vendors, misspellings), dismissal is one click and **dismissals are remembered per case** so the engine doesn't re-ask every draft. **Spec ruling only.** **Build home PARKED 2026-08-08**, unattached: ruled, awaiting a build home; likely lands with the CD-1 build or the intake pipeline, whichever comes first. (Slice A, its loose prior attachment, was withdrawn.) **RE-PARKED 2026-08-11: FE-2 lands with the INTAKE PIPELINE**, superseding "whichever comes first" — reason: the sweep operates on document-name columns and nothing in the CD-1 slice (or the app today) ingests documents, so the parked ruling's premise does not hold here. **The 2026-08-07 ruling itself is untouched** — this moves the home only, it does not reopen the ruling. See `docs/specs/cd1-build-slice.md`.

- ⬜ **FE-5 — Interrogatory count budget: live count per set against a configurable cap by discovery level; document-identification-only interrogatories excluded from the count; soft warning where an interrogatory has facially severable subparts. How aggressive should subpart detection be — attempt facial detection (multiple sentences, enumerated clauses) and show a worst-case count beside the numbered count, accepting false positives on compound-but-unified interrogatories, or warn only on request?** Entered 2026-08-11 (REQ-CAPTURE §5.2). Legal substrate — TRCP 190.3(b)(3), 190.4 — VERIFIED by Michael 2026-08-11; counts may display as facts. Design risk on record: warning fatigue. **RULED 2026-08-11 — design at `form-engine.md` §13.2:** the middle path between this entry's two poles — subpart detection is always-on and purely facial, the worst-case count displays **passively** beside the numbered count, and it escalates to a warning **only when the worst-case crosses the cap while the numbered count does not**, which honors the recorded warning-fatigue risk. Severability judgment stays the attorney's. Question text retained above; **build remains gated on the form engine being named and authorized as a slice, after the CD-1 build per `docs/specs/cd1-build-slice.md`.** **SPEC LANDED 2026-08-15 (#81): `docs/specs/fe-5-interrogatory-budget-spec-2026-08-15.md`** — and **this row's own legal substrate is now under flag.** The line above records TRCP 190.3(b)(3) as "VERIFIED by Michael 2026-08-11; counts may display as facts" — **the spec finds that the verified entry's wording states a different budget than the rule text (Q-FE5-9), so "counts may display as facts" is only as sound as the wording behind it.** Three further scope findings ride below: the cap is **pairwise and cumulative** (per propounding/responding pair, across waves), **Level 1 also caps RFPs and RFAs at 15 each** while FE-5 as ruled counts interrogatories only (Q-FE5-1), and **Level 3 has no cap to look up** — it is an order value, so `cap = f(level)` is structurally wrong there. **The row stays ⬜.**

Sub-question never asked in the capture: does each standalone instrument repeat the definitions block or incorporate it by reference?** Entered 2026-08-11 (REQ-CAPTURE). Combined-mode layout fully evidenced (caption → title → TO → preamble → definitions → instructions → interrogatories → RFPs → RFAs → signature → COS, plus the template's page-break structure); separate mode needs its own layout ruling. **RULED 2026-08-11 — design at `form-engine.md` §13.3:** packaging is a render-time choice from one item model, and the never-asked sub-question is answered — **each standalone instrument REPEATS the definitions block in full, no incorporation by reference** (served documents self-prove; FE-4's versioning removes repetition's drift cost). Separate-mode layout derives from the evidenced combined layout, one COS per document, verification page rides the interrogatories instrument only. Question text retained above; **build remains gated on the form engine being named and authorized as a slice, after the CD-1 build per `docs/specs/cd1-build-slice.md`.** **SPEC LANDED 2026-08-15 (#81): `docs/specs/fe-6-instrument-packaging-spec-2026-08-15.md`** — elaborates §13.3 and restates **FE-17's from-birth annotation as a pointer, not an amendment**: FE-17 *"rides whichever slice creates the ITEM table (the discovery slice), in the same commit as that table,"* so whoever builds FE-6 is bound under a different item's number. Seven new open questions ride below, the sharpest being **Q-FE6-4** — an RFA whose wording flips with the target's relationship to a named party is unmodelled by the three ruled scope values, which say *which targets get an item*, not *which wording a target gets*. **The row stays ⬜.**

### Form corpus (FC series) — ADDED 2026-08-18 (#106)

**The fifteen-item FC block was adjudicated 2026-08-18 (design session, Fable 5); thirteen items were RULED and are recorded at `docs/specs/fc-adjudication-record-2026-08-18.md`, with FC-1–FC-4 folded into `form-engine.md` §3 the same batch. The two rows below are the DEFERRED pair — the only FC items still awaiting Michael.** Governing principle ruled mid-block, his words: *"My system should stay true to one convention as much as possible"* — `{token}` is the one convention, and every other corpus notation is a legacy import artifact, never written back.

- ✅ **`FC-12` — Import posture: is any of the four-library form corpus (MDB PLLC, Team Brennan, Old Office Files, Goldman; ~33,800 files) ever imported into the form engine as templates at all?** The options as put: **(a)** hand-authored clean templates only, with the corpus kept as reference; **(b)** import with a mandatory scrub gate; **(c)** import structure only — skeletons, section orders, clause lists — and never body text. Evidence weighed at the put: **nothing in any library is safely blank** (mining doc §8), and **six of the fifteen tokenized letters hard-code prior content.** **DEFERRED 2026-08-18 (#106), bare — his word: "Defer." No revisit trigger was named.** **CONSERVATIVE DEFAULT GOVERNS WHILE THIS IS OPEN: NO CORPUS CONTENT IMPORTS.** Deferral is not import authorization. FE-D1 is unaffected — its §11.3 skeleton extraction works from Michael’s own shell per `form-engine.md` §8, not from the mined corpus. — **OPEN, deferred; yours** **RULED 2026-08-18 (#108) — "(c) Structure only," after Michael asked for and received a plain-terms explanation and picked on the re-put. The four-library corpus may contribute **skeletons, section orders and clause lists — NEVER body text**; body prose is always written clean. This replaces the interim no-imports default. **No import tooling is authorized to build by this ruling** — that is a later, separately-authorized act. CLOSED.**
- ✅ **`FC-13` — Do any of the corpus’s legal propositions become registry entries, and which?** The candidates as put: **Prop. Code ch. 55** (hospital / EMS liens); **CPRC ch. 146** timely billing, including the **2025 (c-1) amendment**; **Rule 47 tier bands**; **§ 18.001 / § 41.0105** affidavit mechanics; **TDRPC 1.04(f)** fee-splitting. **DEFERRED 2026-08-18 (#106) — his word, in full: "We should defer all of these for now and see if we can resolve any of them by running through the ‘Knowledge Repo’ folder to see which of them can be confirmed through the exact official texts of rules that are contained in there."** **The directed pass RAN the same session** (record §4): TDRPC 1.08(g) and 1.04(f)(2) match their citations as read; TRCP 239a is a notice rule (the SCRA question stays federal and outside all channels); TRCP 47(c)’s $250k bands and discovery bar confirmed; TRCP 194a confirmed for Family Code suits. **ALL OF THAT IS RETRIEVAL AND ALL OF IT IS UNVERIFIED — no registry entry was created, no Status line moved, and only Michael verifies.** The row holds the open half: **whether any proposition opens as an entry at all, one at a time, your call.** — **OPEN, deferred; yours** **RULED 2026-08-18 (#108), BOTH LIMBS. First limb (multi-select, all four picked): registry entries OPEN for **Prop. Code ch. 55** (hospital/EMS liens), **CPRC ch. 146 including the 2025 (c-1) amendment**, **TRCP 47(c) bands**, and **§ 18.001 / § 41.0105**. Second limb: **"Open 1.04(f) entry"** — TDRPC 1.04(f) opens as the registry's FIRST practice-of-law proposition, drafted from the State Bar clean-authority PDF. All five are **drafting acts for a later Opus session** — SOURCING-compliant, source named per item, under the standing verbatim-drafting direction — and every one enters **UNVERIFIED**; opening is not verification. **Michael chose the plain open over the offered open-plus-boundary-ruling variant, so NO standing ruling on the cases-vs-practice registry boundary was made** — that question (`Q-STAT-5` stack) stays open and undecided. CLOSED; the five drafting acts are pending.**

### Contact directory (CD series) — ADDED 2026-08-08

**New heading, Code's call per the packet's §4.2** — the file already sections §7 by series (FE, PL), so a CD heading fits the structure rather than fighting it. Placed directly beneath the FE series because CD-1 supersedes FE-1's framing.

- ⬜ **CD-1 — One global contact directory as the identity source for the form engine and all case-linked people/entities; provider is a role on it. How does it relate to `case_parties` and `case_clients` — views over one directory, or separate tables with links?** Living spec — revisited as modules surface new field needs. Needs its own design session with schema on screen. NOT authorized for build. Issued 2026-08-08, superseding FE-1's provider framing. **Path RULED 2026-08-11:** the session fires after a BOUNDED evidence pass — opportunistic REQ-CAPTUREs continue, plus ONE deliberate roster-mining pass over the document bank (run in PI DISCOVERY, Michael's hand) — then the typed session with schema on screen. Prep scaffold: `docs/specs/cd-1-session-prep.md`. **CONSTRAINT (roster capture REQ-14, ruled 2026-08-12): sides are a property of the case type, not a constant — any roster model with exactly two sides fails on documents the practice demonstrably files (interpleader, certificates of interested parties, scire facias, ex parte petitions, ad litem joinder by court action). GATE MET 2026-08-12: the bounded evidence pass of #48 ruling 4 is complete — the roster-mining capture is reconciled and ruled; the schema session fires at Michael's choosing. SESSION QUESTIONS added from the roster capture, full text in `docs/specs/REQ-CAPTURE_roster-mining-pass_2026-08-11.md` §5.1 (probate roster modeling: defer entirely, or reserve now for the estate-representative/heirs pattern the PI documents already prove), §5.2 (non-party actors with roles in scope from day one, or parties-only first — the mined evidence says parties-only misses the people drafting revolves around; the gate finding, REQ-15), §5.3 (attorneys-of-record in the same directory with a role, or a separate structure), §5.5 (identity resolution across roles/cases/aliases at CD-1, or per-case identity for the first consumer).** **RULED 2026-08-11 — the schema session ran; every CD-1 question was ruled item by item. Spec: `docs/specs/contact-directory.md` (LIVING SPEC — revisited as modules surface new field needs; additions to its §5/§6 controlled vocabularies are spec-level acts). The core fork resolves as SEPARATE TABLES WITH LINKS, the shape already built: `parties` IS the directory, `case_parties` stays the roster link, `case_clients` stays parallel, D-CL2-8 untouched. Session questions are answered in the spec: §2 scope (non-parties in from day one; attorneys in the same directory, attorney is a role; probate reserves the PI-proven estate-adjacent pattern only), §3.3 identity resolution (none at CD-1 — cross-case identity is inherent in the one-global-row shape; merge-two-contacts is a named future need). BUILD STILL NOT AUTHORIZED — no table, no migration, no UI, no `party_type` migration. This entry stays open as the living-spec pointer.** *(The prep scaffold `docs/specs/cd-1-session-prep.md` was absorbed by the spec and deleted the same day, per its own delete-or-absorb line.)* **BUILD SLICE AUTHORIZED 2026-08-11 within the scope of `docs/specs/cd1-build-slice.md`** — seven IN items (parties evolution; `case_parties` evolution with flags-never-guesses backfill per the CL-2 pattern; roster definitions as data, seeded only for bank-evidenced case types; the contact-edges table; directory UI including the scope-label edit surface; RLS + probe extension from birth; live migration by Michael's hand). Explicit OUTs: form engine, IN-2 fact table, merge tooling, service-story fields, probate beyond the reserved pattern, the `/rules` seed. **The spec remains the design authority; living-spec posture unchanged; anything outside that slice's scope remains unauthorized.** The build is a separate Code session, not the queue runner.
- ⬜ **CD-2 — Case-type party rosters + typed contact relationships (the "family" layer). Each case type carries an expected roster of party roles presented as slots at intake — never auto-created records (trucking PI: carrier / driver / lessor / trainer / parent; premises: owner / occupier / management; probate: decedent / personal representative / heirs / creditors; criminal: defendant / complainant / prosecution) — and directory contacts carry typed edges (parent/subsidiary, employer/employee, spouse, heir-of). Role tags drive downstream content selection: the same RFA flips phrasing depending on whether the target IS the tractor owner or a sibling of it, so tags are template selectors, not labels. How do rosters attach to the case-type tree, and how do contact edges relate to the directory schema?** FRAMING RULED 2026-08-11 (general across practice areas — trucking corporate edges and probate family edges are one structure; roster mechanism belongs to the case-type model, not a trucking feature later abstracted). Design UNRULED — input to the CD-1 session. NOT authorized for build. Distinct from CL-1: `case_links` links cases; this links contacts — a CD-1 session names both so nobody merges them later. Evidence: REQ-CAPTURE 2026-08-11 (trucking first sets); probate is the second real consumer. **EVIDENCE LANDED 2026-08-12: the roster-mining capture (`docs/specs/REQ-CAPTURE_roster-mining-pass_2026-08-11.md`) — per-type constellations REQ-01–10, typed-relationship inventory REQ-11, capacity multiplicity REQ-12 (party = person × capacity), expected/optional/rare slot frequencies REQ-13, service-path-as-party-attribute REQ-19 (cross-link IN-4). TTCA roster default (attorney practice note, 2026-08-12): governmental body alone in nearly all cases; employee joinder deliberate and exceptional.** **RULED 2026-08-11 into the spec — BOTH layers. This entry becomes a pointer; the evidence annotations above stay.** Roster layer → `docs/specs/contact-directory.md` §4: slot definitions are data on the case-type tree with inheritance and expectancy tiers (expected / optional / rare-with-procedure), never auto-creating records; the roster entry decomposes into role / caption alignment (drawn from the case type's defined side set — REQ-14 satisfied by construction, "Plaintiff" is an alignment, not a role) / party status / firm perspective (the existing `side` column's true meaning); entries are history, not snapshot (joined-by + active state, because FE-8 and IN-4 both need roster-as-of). Relationship layer → §5: one directional typed edge structure with optional case scope (null = world fact), a controlled extensible vocabulary seeded from REQ-11, the one-home rule (capacity references stay on the roster entry and never auto-create edges), and the **CL-1 firewall named** — contact edges and `case_links` never merge, never share a structure, and neither ever holds the other's kind of link (the D-CL1 items stay untouched and unruled). **NOT authorized for build.** **EVIDENCE ADDED 2026-08-12 (#58), CD-1/CD-2 both, full text per QR-1 — two annotations, no new IDs:** (a) *registered agent as entity-level data* (UIM capture REQ-07, same home as roster REQ-19's service-path-as-party-attribute) — the service paragraph's agent name and address were inherited from an exemplar and had to be independently checked, so registered-agent name and address are stored per entity with a **verify-before-service state and a staleness date, never copied as prose**; (b) *verification-officer tracking* (deficiency capture REQ-11, priority someday) — **track who verified which response set (contact × role) and cross-reference against fact-witness roles**, live evidence being that the same officer verified interrogatory answers and personally conducted the investigation those answers denied, a deposition/credibility lever. (b) is carried at full length deliberately: it is the K-6/K-7 cheap insurance. **EVIDENCE ADDED 2026-08-12 (#62), from the Uvalde capture, no new IDs:** (c) *the county prosecutor roster* — contacts with roles, with collision-safe short codes, is directory substrate; the requirement itself is **CR-2**; (d) *contacts-at-capture is SATISFIED BY CONSTRUCTION* — "contacts with roles attach to the matter at capture" is the built directory plus roster link (log #61, items 1–2), so it took no ID at all; its print-forward half rides **CR-9**. **(e) ADDED 2026-08-12 (#64): carrier-ID and financial-responsibility TYPE codes from the CR-3 code sheet are directory-vocabulary CANDIDATES** — PROPOSED only, pointer at `docs/specs/cr3-field-code-map.md`. **Adding directory vocabulary is a spec-level act on the living spec (the CD-3 precedent), so nothing was added.**
- ✅ **`party_type` RETENTION — CONFIRMED by Michael 2026-08-12 (#62).** The CD-1 build **retained** `party_type` where the slice text said "retiring" it, and disclosed the deviation in its #61 handback. **Confirmed, not merely accepted:** dropping the column meant rebuilding registry field rendering, which is outside the slice's scope; role tags supersede it as the thing the app filters on; the tag vocabulary is **derived** from the party-type registry so the two cannot drift; and `role_tags[0]` mirrors it. `docs/specs/cd1-build-slice.md` carries a dated qualification note — **the original slice text stands; nothing was silently rewritten.**
- ⬜ **CD-3 — Insurer entity subtype (county mutual / foreign insurer / domestic company) as directory-level data, with a flag raised whenever the defendant slot changes and citizenship/diversity implications follow (live evidence: the exemplar's defendant paragraph pleaded Texas citizenship — accurate for its county-mutual defendant, inaccurate and removal-relevant for a foreign insurer in the new defendant slot). Sub-question (UIM capture §5 Q5): is entity-type-driven removal-exposure flagging — a foreign insurer in the defendant slot plus a relief bracket above the federal jurisdictional threshold — in scope for the software's deadline/strategy engine, or is that attorney-only analysis the software should merely annotate?** Entered 2026-08-12 (UIM-UDJA REQ-CAPTURE, priority soon). **Flag-only per registry discipline — the software annotates, the attorney analyzes.** Additions to `contact-directory.md`'s §5/§6 controlled vocabularies are spec-level acts under its living-spec posture, so the subtype vocabulary is not written by a Code session. **NOT authorized for build.**

*(**CD-4 – CD-13 ADDED 2026-08-13, twenty-ninth queue-runner invocation**, from the CD-2 role mining pass — `docs/specs/cd2-role-mining-pass-2026-08-13.md`, PROPOSED design input, data prep only. The packet carried them as packet-local **Q1–Q10** and explicitly declined to mint a series, leaving ID assignment to the runner. **Collision check run repo-wide before assignment: only CD-1, CD-2, CD-3 existed anywhere in `docs/`, so CD-4+ is a continuation of the existing series, not a new one** — which is what the packet's §7 named as the natural home. Mapping for anyone tracing the packet: Q1→CD-4, Q2→CD-5, Q3→CD-6, Q4→CD-7, Q5→CD-8, Q6→CD-9, Q7→CD-10, Q8→CD-11, Q9→CD-12, Q10→CD-13. Full question text carried per QR-1 — the packet is deleted after processing, so these rows are the only place the questions survive. **None is authorized for build; the mining pass rules nothing and the CD-2 row above stays a pointer.**)*

- ⬜ **CD-4 — Should the two source files that hold the authoritative role vocabulary — `src/domain/partyRegistry.ts` and `src/domain/roster.ts` — be pasted into a design chat so the CD-2 mining pass can mark BUILT vs. CANDIDATE with certainty instead of `TAG-CHECK: NOT RUN`? The project instructions bar design sessions from reading `src/` and name pasting as the escape hatch; roughly 60 rows in the pass's §3 turn on it. Alternatively: should a Code session run the comparison instead, since Code reads `src/` freely?** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q1). **Blocks precision on ~60 rows.** The design session had bridge access to both files this session and **deliberately did not open them**, treating bridge access as no repeal of the design-side `src/` bar — recorded because the restraint is the reason the gap is honest rather than papered over. **The packet's §6 forbids a Code session running the comparison unprompted:** converting `TAG-CHECK: NOT RUN` into resolved rows changes a design-side artifact's evidentiary basis, and this row offers it as an option Michael has not chosen. Two words close it either way.
- ⬜ **CD-5 — Is a role that is defined by the ABSENCE of another role — probate's "interested person" (§352.052(c) excludes creditors and claimants) and property law's "derivative claimant" ("a claimant other than an original contractor", §53.056) — expressible in the ruled roster model at all, or does it need a new construct?** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q2). Two independent practice areas produced the same shape from unrelated statutes, which argues it is **structural rather than incidental**; nothing in the built vocabulary expresses it. Practical consequence named by the pass: a generic "interested party" contact label **silently admits the excluded classes** — the exclusion IS the entry.
- ⬜ **CD-6 — Should `case_parties.role` remain FREE TEXT? It is the axis the whole taxonomy is about, and it is the only one of the six ruled axes with no CHECK constraint, no enum, and no lookup table — `party_status`, `capacity_kind`, `joined_by`, `active_state`, and the 19 edge types are all DB-enforced; `role` is not.** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q3). **The packet's §6 forbids a Code session adding a CHECK constraint to that column** — this is Michael's ruling, not a cleanup. Related fact from the same pass: `role_tags` likewise has no enum, no CHECK, and no lookup table; its vocabulary is enforced **entirely in TypeScript** (a derivation guarantee, not a database one).
- ⬜ **CD-7 — Should the asymmetric contact-edge types get their inverse directions — `lessee-of` to match `lessor-of`, `bailor-of` to match `bailee-of`? Adding an edge type is a spec-level act, so this is a ruling, not a fix.** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q4). **The packet's §6 forbids "fixing" the one-directional edges** — the absences are deliberate pending this ruling. Only one direction of each asymmetric edge exists in the built 19.
- ⬜ **CD-8 — Should `parent-of` be split? It currently serves both corporate parent/subsidiary (the trucking holding-company family) and human parent/child (the next-friend case). One edge type, two ontologies.** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q5). **The packet's §6 forbids resolving the overload** by splitting, aliasing, or adding a discriminator.
- ⬜ **CD-9 — Four separate party types (Business, Insurance company, Government entity, Provider business) each carry their own `registered agent (name & address)` field, while `registered-agent-of` also exists as an edge type. That is duplicated identity across types — the condition the enter-once principle exists to prevent. Consolidate onto the edge, or keep the fields?** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q6). Cross-links the CD-2 evidence annotation (a): registered-agent name and address are stored **per entity with a verify-before-service state and a staleness date, never copied as prose** — whichever way this rules, that constraint rides along.
- ⬜ **CD-10 — Do internal firm actors (responsible attorney, assigned paralegal, timekeeper, the ReviewLog "who") live in the SAME person namespace as parties, or in a separate auth/user namespace?** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q7). The case-event core's `actor` field and the time facet's `timekeeper` both point at this unresolved namespace, and **the system is single-user today with staff later** — which is exactly the condition under which the wrong answer is cheap now and expensive later.
- ⬜ **CD-11 — On the criminal side, are "the State," "the DA's office," and the individual prosecutor one entity at three resolutions, or three records?** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q8). The record uses all three **interchangeably and never reconciles them**, while CR-7's three-way number mapping implies the DA's office holds its own identity namespace per cause. Cross-links CR-2 §5 Q1 (prosecutor as a cause-level role needing a provenance/confidence dimension no other contact role needs).
- ⬜ **CD-12 — Does BONDSMAN attach at cause level or matter level? The Uvalde capture lists it among fields "needed per cause/matter" without resolving which — bonds are written per cause, but a defendant may use one bondsman across causes. (Same question, third instance: does "client's mother" attach per matter or per client? A client with three causes has one mother.)** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q9). This is the **same ambiguity CR-2 §5 Q1 flags for prosecutor, and it was not flagged in the capture** — the pass caught it. Mirror-image datum from the same pass: jail/correctional facility is **client-level, not cause-level**.
- ⬜ **CD-13 — Is there a path for an ORGANIZATIONAL CLIENT? The Client party type is built entirely around an individual (DOB, SSN, driver's license, emergency contact, health insurer), while the Servpro restoration operator is a business and business plaintiffs exist on the civil line.** Entered 2026-08-13 (CD-2 mining pass §5 Q10). Related and already on the record as an open spec-feedback item: **the party-type promotion path (PNC → Client) does not exist**, so re-entering a person as a new Client party would split their cross-case history.

### Intake pipeline (IN series) — ADDED 2026-08-11

*(New heading by the same structural logic as the CD heading, #39. FE-2's parked build home already names "the intake pipeline" as a candidate landing zone; IN-1 and IN-2 arriving makes it look like a real module — drift named, not decided. **Elicitation caveat, kept on the record: the IN-series naming rode inside ruling 1's "Yes on 1"; if Michael meant FE numbers instead, renaming is a one-word veto before these IDs get load-bearing.** Placement note — the fold-in said "after the FE series," but the file documents at the CD heading that CD sits directly beneath FE deliberately, because CD-1 supersedes FE-1's framing; the IN heading therefore lands after the CD series rather than splitting that documented adjacency.)*

- ⬜ **IN-1 — Answer-mining for discovery hooks: when a defendant's filed answer is ingested, flag affirmative pleadings and capacity denials that support contention discovery and suggest request stubs (live evidence: an alternative-benefits-policy assertion and a not-proper-party denial each generated a contention interrogatory + RFP + RFAs). Is answer ingestion a distinct pipeline step, and where in the UI do suggestions surface?** Entered 2026-08-11 (REQ-CAPTURE §5.3). FE-2's rulings carry as PROPOSED defaults: flags for Michael, never auto-add; one-click dismissal; dismissals remembered per case. Honest capability note: recognizing "this paragraph pleads an affirmative defense" is document understanding, not string matching — the Phase-1b local-LLM capability class, and the spec should say so. **SPEC LANDED 2026-08-15 (#83): `docs/specs/in-1-answer-mining-spec-2026-08-15.md`** — and it lands in the OPPOSITE posture from the Task 8 form-engine specs. FE-4/FE-5/FE-6 were open because the **build** was gated while the design was RULED at `form-engine.md` §13; **IN-1 has no ruling to elaborate** — this row asks the design question in terms and nothing on the record answers it. **The spec therefore MAPS the design space and hands the question back; it says so on its face, and a build session must not read it as a design to implement.** **The row stays ⬜: a spec is not a closure.** Three findings ride below as Q-IN1-1..9. **IN-1 has no design-doc home at all** — word-bounded at HEAD it appears 16 times across 6 files (BUILD-STATE, this queue, the log, three REQ-CAPTUREs) and **in no design document**. **The four PROPOSED defaults on the line above are RULED for FE-2 only** — `form-engine.md` line 236 is their sole canonical statement and it rules them for FE-2's document-name sweep in a disclosures draft, not for mining a filed pleading; two restatements on the record are lossy (`cd1-build-slice.md` line 62 drops *"one click"*). **And the IN-6-as-substrate claim cannot be reconciled** (Q-IN1-3) — IN-6 parses a served *response set*, IN-1's input is a filed *answer*: different documents, different procedural moments, so IN-1 cannot silently inherit IN-6's parser or entity shape.
- ⬜ **IN-2 — Crash-report field extraction + discrepancy flags: structured extraction of CR-3 fields (VINs, carrier identity, DOT number, owner/lessee, driver-license class codes) into the case record, plus automatic flags when petition allegations and crash-report fields diverge (live evidence: two real conflicts — incident time; pleaded vehicle ownership). Implies per-field SOURCE ATTRIBUTION on the case record ("this VIN came from the crash report; the petition says otherwise") — a data-model decision touching CD-1 (vehicle/instrumentality identity) and the intake spec, not a feature bolt-on. Where does source attribution live?** Entered 2026-08-11 (REQ-CAPTURE). Extraction half is Phase-1b-class ingestion (easiest possible target — fixed state form — but still PDF ingestion, GPU-arm gated). **EXTENDED 2026-08-12 (attorney-edit REQ-CAPTURE, ruled — no new ID, one home per question per the CL2-AC-1 dedup principle): generalized to source-fact pinning. Case facts used in any instrument are first-class data — {fact_id, value, source_document, source_field, extraction_method, verified_by_attorney} — referenced by merge fields, so every instrument can be re-verified mechanically and a corrected fact propagates to every instrument that uses it. Image-only sources (scanned forms with no text layer) take OCR or vision extraction with a confidence flag. Sub-question (capture §5.3): what confidence threshold or human-confirmation step gates a "fact verified" state, and should an unverifiable fact block the finalized state (interacts with IN-4's lifecycle)?** **HOME QUESTION RULED 2026-08-11 — `docs/specs/contact-directory.md` §7: source attribution lives on the CASE-SCOPED FACT TABLE ({fact_id, value, source_document, source_field, extraction_method, verified_by_attorney}), not on directory entries and not in a sidecar (FE-1c's silent-trust posture is preserved; a sidecar would erect a second provenance regime over data ruled to need none). Identity-class facts (a carrier's DOT number, a facility's legal name) stage on the fact table and promote into the directory entry ONLY by attorney verification — one direction of flow, no copies. Confidence scores order the review queue and drive display flags; NO threshold ever auto-verifies (registry discipline transposed to facts: automation flags, only Michael verifies), which answers the confidence half of the §5.3 sub-question. Discrepancy flags fall out of the shape — two sources asserting different values for one fact row IS the flag.** **STILL OPEN: the EXTRACTION half** — structured CR-3 field extraction remains Phase-1b-class ingestion, GPU-arm gated, and the question text above is retained in full because none of it was ruled. **The block-finalized sub-question ("should an unverifiable fact block the finalized instrument state") now RIDES IN-4** — deliberately not ruled in the CD-1 session. **EVIDENCE ADDED 2026-08-12 (#64): the authoritative CR-3 field/code inventory is filed at `docs/specs/cr3-field-code-map.md`** (synthesis, PROPOSED design input) **with the raw code sheet at `docs/reference/CR3-code-sheet-2023.pdf`** (Form CR-3CS 4/1/2023, governs wrecks 2023-forward). **The load-bearing requirement it carries: crash-report code tables are VERSIONED EXTERNAL VOCABULARIES.** The 2023+ sheet supersedes earlier sheets, so extraction must store **(code, decoded label, code-sheet version)** and the tables must carry effective dates — otherwise a 2022 wreck's codes decode against 2023 meanings and nothing signals it. The extraction design consumes this; **IN-2 stays queued and unauthorized.** **SPEC LANDED 2026-08-15 (#84): `docs/specs/in-2-crash-report-extraction-spec-2026-08-15.md`** — and its gate resolves **BOTH ways**, unlike either predecessor in this chain: the **HOME** half is RULED at `contact-directory.md` §7 and the spec **elaborates** it; the **EXTRACTION** half is unruled and the spec **only maps** it. The spec's §1.1 names which of its sections do which, so a build session cannot read a mapped paragraph as a ruled one. **The row stays ⬜: a spec is not a closure.** Nine findings ride below as Q-IN2-1..9. **The structural finding: IN-2's five named targets are THREE extraction problems and the #64 versioned-code-table requirement governs only ONE** — coded fields (license class, carrier ID type) take versioned vocabularies; **VINs and the DOT number have no code list at all** and take format validation; carrier and owner identity take §7.2's promotion gate. **Three of the five are not class A**, so a build that implements #64 alone ships with no VIN validation and no promotion gate, silently. **Two lines of `cr3-field-code-map.md` are corrected in the spec and NEITHER file was edited:** list 38 has **70** enumerated values, not the *"(79 codes)"* stated — 79 is the highest code number, the space is sparse (5–13 and 72 absent), and **range validation is therefore wrong**; and the demographic question that file calls open **was RULED at #66**, which names *"CR-3 extraction"* as a governed intake path, making fields 17/18 the only IN-2 fields whose scope is ruled. **Also newly on the record: `oaa_intakes.fields_json` already carries "value/confidence/provenance per field"** — precedent for the shape, **not a substrate** (a `text` blob on an intake event, no `verified_by_attorney`, and two sources cannot disagree inside it); **`cases.date_of_incident` is a `date`, so the incident-time conflict the row itself cites has no column to conflict with**; and **vehicle identity has no home anywhere** — `parties.kind` admits only individual/organization, and word-bounded `vin`/`vehicle`/`instrumentality` return ZERO across the schema and all three migrations.
- ⬜ **IN-3 — Held sets with service triggers: a document/set status of HOLD with a defined trigger event (answer filed / appearance) that surfaces a finalize-and-serve prompt (live evidence: a full driver set drafted pre-appearance with placeholder COS). Trigger source PRE-ANSWERED for v1: MANUAL attorney input only, per the standing Q-6 posture — docket-watch is CourtListener integration, which is UNAUTHORIZED; the upgrade path routes through Q-6 whenever that is revisited, so this never becomes a second unguarded door into the same integration. What does the HOLD lifecycle look like in the document model?** Entered 2026-08-11 (REQ-CAPTURE §5.4). Cross-link 2026-08-12: IN-4 now holds the full instrument lifecycle; IN-3's HOLD/trigger stands separate as the pre-service fragment. **SPEC LANDED 2026-08-15 (#83): `docs/specs/in-3-held-sets-service-triggers-spec-2026-08-15.md`** — same posture as IN-1's: **no ruling to elaborate**, so the spec maps the design space and hands the question back. **The row stays ⬜.** Nine findings ride below as Q-IN3-1..9. **HOLD cannot be expressed on a document today:** `generated_documents` has **no status column of any kind**, its `doc_type` CHECK admits one value, and it has **no set/parent grouping column** — so the "set" in "held set" has no structural existence either; the token `held` exists twice in the schema (`staging_items.status`, `routing_decisions.action`), both on the transcript inbox, neither on a document. **And nothing can fire the trigger** — no case-event table, no docket table, no `appearance` column, and neither `case_parties.joined_by` nor `calendar_events.event_type` carries an `'answer'`/`'appearance'` value. **The two trigger facts were already observed at party level** — the origin capture's entity-level merge fields carry `{{answer_filed_date}}` and `{{appeared}}` (bool), evidence that trigger state belongs on the roster entry next to the service story `contact-directory.md` §6.2 already puts there. **Evidence, not a decision (Q-IN3-5).** **Sharpest scheduling consequence: Q-IN3-3** — `cd1-build-slice.md` and the CD-1 migration both deferred the service-story columns to *"the first instrument consumer"* and **that consumer has never been named**; IN-3 and IN-4 are both candidates, and CD-1's living-spec revisit opens on whichever is named.
- ⬜ **IN-4 — Instrument lifecycle states: generated → delivered → attorney-edited → finalized → served, each with timestamp and file provenance; the certificate-of-service date is bound to the SERVICE EVENT — stamped at service time, never typed into the text — and drives downstream deadline computation (live evidence: a hand-filled certificate date across five documents goes silently stale if service slips a day). Sub-question (attorney-edit capture §5.4): how does the system handle mixed service across a document family (some recipients served one day, others later) — one certificate per recipient with per-recipient dates?** Entered 2026-08-12 (attorney-edit REQ-CAPTURE). Cross-links: IN-3 (HOLD + service trigger — the pre-service lifecycle fragment; stands separate); the queued deadline-engine input (per-defendant service dates within one wave, each response deadline computed independently); roster REQ-19 (service path as a party attribute).
- ⬜ **IN-5 — Disclosure-mining: when the other side's disclosures are ingested, extract the four categories the attorney reads them for — identities of responsible third parties alleged by the other side; identities of witnesses or persons with knowledge of the claims and defenses; insuring agreements; identities and/or production of documents that side intends to use in the prosecution/defense of their case — feeding the contact roster and the case record. Where do the extracted hooks surface, and how do they interact with IN-1's answer-mining suggestions?** Entered 2026-08-12, sourced to Michael's direct statement in the design session (not a REQ-CAPTURE). Sibling of IN-1 (same flags-only, never-auto-add defaults carry as PROPOSED). Cross-links: CD-1's non-party scope question (three of the four categories are non-party actors); the "who is whose insurer" relationship (roster REQ-06). Legal substrate: the expanded TRCP 194 registry entry (VERIFIED 2026-08-12). Document-understanding capability class, Phase-1b-adjacent, like IN-1.
- ⬜ **IN-6 — Response-set ingestion: parse a served response set into structured items (instrument type, number, request text, objections[], answer text) — the mirror image of the incoming-request ingestion requirement already observed on the responding side (live evidence: the opponent's response documents restate each request followed by objections and an answer, and building the deficiency grid required manually decomposing about 60 items).** Entered 2026-08-12 (deficiency-handling REQ-CAPTURE, priority now). This is **the substrate IN-1's answer-mining runs on** — without structured response items there is nothing for answer-mining to mine. Cross-links IN-1, IN-5. Entity shape observed by the capture: `response_set` {set_number, instrument_mix, served_date, verifier} and `response_item` {instrument_type: rog|rfp|rfa, number, request_text, objections[], answer_text, categories[], evidence_refs[], cure_demand, outbound: bool, cluster}.
- ⬜ **IN-7 — Cross-document contradiction detection: link answer assertions to contrary evidence in the produced record; even manual flagging with structured links would capture most of the value. Live evidence — the highest-value deficiencies were contradictions between the opponent's answers and its OWN production and disclosures: an answer denying any investigation while produced statement forms recite an investigation on their face; an admission denial contradicted by a same-day, same-declarant interrogatory answer; a persons-with-knowledge disclosure omitting people named in the opponent's own documents.** Entered 2026-08-12 (deficiency-handling REQ-CAPTURE, priority soon). **Manual structured links first**, per the capture's own instinct. Document-understanding capability class, Phase-1b-adjacent, like IN-1/IN-5; **flags only, never conclusions.**

### Deficiency enforcement (DE series) — ADDED 2026-08-12

*(**Series RULED into existence by Michael, 2026-08-12**, in the same group ruling that assigned the deficiency capture's durable IDs — the anchor item fits neither FE (not rendering) nor IN (not intake), which is the **WF-1 structural precedent**; a WF home was offered and declined. Placement is Code's call and is stated rather than buried: the file sections §7 by series, and DE lands directly after the IN series because **its own substrate is an IN item** — DE-1's item store is what IN-6's response-set ingestion feeds — so the reading order FE → CD → IN → DE follows the data, with WF/DL/PL/H after. **The #48 elicitation caveat applies to DE exactly as it does to IN: renaming is a one-word veto until these IDs get load-bearing.**)*

- ⬜ **DE-1 — Deficiency taxonomy as first-class data: a classification vocabulary applied per item — boilerplate objection (BP); answer given "subject to" objections (STO); objection-only with no substantive response (OO); evasive or incomplete (EV); contradicted by the responder's own documents (CONTRA); privilege asserted without the required withholding procedure (PRIV); and RFP responses stating none of the rule's permitted response forms — driving downstream rendering. It is the enforcement-side sibling of the objection library already observed on the responding side. ABSORBS the module's shape (capture REQ-04): one item store with renderers per instrument — internal working grid (all items), deficiency letter (outbound items, per-item cure paragraphs, doctrinal preamble, conferral designation and fuse), motion chart and proposed order (outbound items grouped by cluster, sustain/overrule column left blank for the court) — with clusters grouped by subject matter rather than seriatim, per the treatise's judge-facing advice. ALSO ABSORBS evidence pinning (capture REQ-08): item-level `evidence_refs[]` resolving to produced-document identifiers and rendered inline in letters and motions, because deficiency assertions gain force by citing the opponent's own production by Bates range. Three questions ride here — (§5 Q1) should the deficiency taxonomy (enforcement side) and the objection library (responding side) be modeled as ONE shared vocabulary viewed from two postures, since an objection pattern we assert when responding is the same object we attack when enforcing, or as two separate libraries that merely cross-reference each other? (§5 Q3) how should motion-chart subject-matter clusters be assigned — manually at enforcement time, or derived from topic tags attached to each request when the set was originally propounded, which would argue for tagging requests at drafting time in the propounding module? (§5 Q6) the treatise recommends multiple small motions to compel, one subject area each, over one omnibus motion — should the motion renderer support splitting one deficiency data set into several instrument instances, and how should the conferral certificate and exhibit lists be shared across them?** Entered 2026-08-12 (deficiency-handling REQ-CAPTURE, priority now). **Series anchor.** Cross-links: **FE-6** (same one-item-model, many-renderings principle on the propounding side — **cross-linked, deliberately NOT merged**: different instrument classes); **IN-2** (source attribution, same family as `evidence_refs[]`). **Design substrate, reconciled as CONFIRMATION rather than collision: the parked discovery-deficiency engine (case-heartbeat walkthrough capture, Part 6 — the Bright treatise closed list, the TRCP 193.2(e) waiver linchpin, the thread-shaped workflow). The CLE treatise this capture's instruments used IS that same paper, and REQ-02's taxonomy is that closed list made data. DE-1/DE-2 are the parked buildout's durable HOME, not its green light — nothing here is authorized for build.**
- ⬜ **DE-2 — Conferral/escalation timeline engine: letter → cure deadline → motion → hearing → compliance deadline, computed as PROPOSALS for attorney verification and integrated with docket-control-order deadlines (live evidence: the letter carries a cure fuse of N days from the letter date; the motion's conference certificate must recite the effort; the agreed DCO imposes a discovery cutoff, a serve-by date of cutoff minus 30 days, expert deadlines, and mediation — and the enforcement track must land compelled production before expert workup needs it). Sub-question (§5 Q2): should the software track the opponent's response-service dates and automatically propose a conferral fuse and escalation timeline, as proposals for attorney confirmation, or should enforcement timelines be created only manually?** Entered 2026-08-12 (deficiency-handling REQ-CAPTURE, priority now). Shape observed: `escalation_timeline` {letter_date, cure_days, cure_deadline, motion_filed, hearing, compliance_deadline}, **all as proposals**. Cross-links: **DL-INPUT** (per-defendant service dates) and **IN-3** (HOLD / service trigger). **Q-6 posture untouched — triggers are MANUAL for v1; no docket-watch.** NOT authorized for build.

### Workflow channels (WF series) — ADDED 2026-08-12

*(New heading, Michael's ruling 2026-08-12: the item fits neither FE — not rendering — nor IN — not intake. Same structural logic as the IN heading in #48.)*

- ⬜ **WF-1 — Review-folder as a workflow channel: a per-matter inbox/outbox convention the software understands — where generated drafts land, where attorney-edited files return — with state transitions inferred from file arrival (live evidence: the attorney's working convention is a dedicated "drafts for review" folder, separate from the generated-output location).** Entered 2026-08-12 (attorney-edit REQ-CAPTURE, priority someday). Kin to the design-side email-workflow requirements doc (PROPOSED, un-packetized). Feeds IN-4's delivered → attorney-edited transition.

*(**WF-2–WF-8 ADDED 2026-08-12 (#63)** by Michael's group ruling adopting `docs/specs/email-workflow-requirements.md` into the repo. Adoption makes the catalog part of the record; **it builds, schedules, and authorizes nothing.** Each row carries its GATE — the thing that must exist or be ruled before the pipeline is buildable — so none can silently die, the K-6/K-7 insurance. The standing carry "email-workflow doc, PROPOSED and un-packetized" **CLOSES** with these.)*

Nothing was inserted into any registry file, nothing was verified, and no `**Status:**` line moved. Carried here with FULL question text per QR-1, because the packet is deleted after processing and these rows are the only surviving copy.** The two records the questions arise from are staged at `docs/specs/fc13-entry-drafts-2026-08-18.md` (DRAFT ENTRY TEXTS — NOT INSERTED) and `docs/specs/ws3-second-pass-read-2026-08-18.md` (READ RECORD — RETRIEVAL ONLY).

**Two of the twelve unblock the most, on the packet’s own read:** `FC13-Q-2` (do the drafts insert at all, and how) and `Q-WS3P-1` (the strongest new authority). **Three of the six `Q-WS3P` items and one `FC13-Q` item carry the same cost and it is stated once here:** the `WS-3` entry and the `## TRCP 47(b)–(d)` entry are both **VERIFIED**, and adopting any candidate wording DETACHES that verification and needs a later re-verification look. **Nothing in this series closes an existing row** — `Q-WS3-5` stays exactly as #108 left it until `Q-WS3P-4` is answered.

- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-1`** — **Are these six drafts, or five?** #108 opens five entries, but its fourth candidate is stated as **"§ 18.001 / § 41.0105"** — one pick covering two statutes. They are drafted as **two separate texts**, because they are different statutes doing different work and one-proposition-one-home points away from a combined entry. If a single combined entry was intended, say so and they merge. Nothing turns on this until insertion. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-2`** — **Do these insert at all, given the reconcile?** #108's own text says the five are "drafted … **inserted** UNVERIFIED," so insertion is the ruled default; this question exists only because every destination turned out to be occupied or absent — facts the ruling did not have. Options, not exclusive across the five: **(a)** insert as new entries and accept the overlap; **(b)** insert as *second observations* on the existing entries, the 194.1(a) pattern; **(c)** treat them as ROUTE-C conforming candidates for the existing entries; **(d)** hold them staged and insert nothing yet. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-3`** — **TRCP 47(c): new entry, or conforming rewording of the VERIFIED entry?** The existing `## TRCP 47(b)–(d)` entry (VERIFIED 2026-08-17) diverges from the operative rule in four ways, two of which change what software built on it would do: **(1)** it says the party seeks "**exactly one** of five specified forms of relief" — the rule does not say "exactly one," the same construction shape as `Q-T19-4`'s art. 27.18 "may accept … if" that you deliberately did not rule; **(2)** it omits "**whether an original petition, counterclaim, cross-claim, or third party claim**" — teeth for FE-14, since a bracket picklist prompting only on an original petition misses counterclaims, cross-claims and third-party claims the rule reaches; **(3)** it omits the alternative-relief and special-exception sentence; **(4)** conformity drift — "that/which sets forth," "deems itself/himself," "its pleading / the party's pleading," "with 47(c) / with (c)." Divergences 1 and 2 change what the entry means, which is the ROUTE-C trigger — **but the entry is VERIFIED and adopting conforming wording DETACHES your 2026-08-17 verification.** Adopt, reject, or edit; if adopted, re-verification is a separate later look. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-4`** — **What is the relationship between these drafts and medical-billing ENTRIES 1, 2, 4 and 5?** Those four already exist as DRAFT entries covering the same four authorities, and **ENTRY 5 already carries the 2025 (c-1) amendment**. They are synthesis-shaped with per-proposition `[READ]`/`[STATUTE]`/`[EXT]`/`[JUDGMENT]` flags; the new texts are verbatim-operative-text-shaped in the backlog files' house form. Do the new texts **supersede** them, **join** them as verbatim Rule lines inside the existing entries, or **coexist** in a different file? **Related and unruled: `Q-STAT-2` asks whether that file's exclusion from the backlog is deliberate at all** — the two may want answering together. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-5`** — **Where does the TDRPC 1.04(f) entry live?** There is no practice-of-law registry file and the registry carries not one professional-conduct proposition. Candidates: **(a)** a new file, e.g. `legal-rule-registry-practice-and-professional-conduct.md`; **(b)** into the enforcement-and-pleading file despite its subject line; **(c)** hold it staged until `Q-STAT-5` is ruled. **No session should pick this — naming a home decides part of the cases-vs-practice boundary you expressly left undecided at #108.** Note that four research passes already stack behind `Q-STAT-5`'s one ruling, so this may be the cheapest moment to take it. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`FC13-Q-6`** — **Should the §3 extraction-method note be amended to cover the reading-order finding — and do you accept that this session already relied on the unamended version?** SOURCING says *every quotation is spot-checked against raw extraction.* **Four of this packet's 27 source quotations do not pass that check as written** — Prop. Code §§ 55.002(c), 55.003(b), 55.005(a) and TRCP 47(b)–(d) — because the raw extraction of those passages is itself corrupted: scrambled reading order, injected page numbers, a displaced subdivision marker, **across three files and two publishers.** They were resolved on a discipline this session wrote: raw governs **character content**, layout governs **reading order**, every disagreement **reported**. That is a candidate amendment to a binding method note; it was **applied and disclosed here, not authorized.** Adopt, reject, or edit — and if you reject it, those four quotations are the ones needing another route. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`Q-WS3P-1`** — **Do you adopt `C-DICK-1`?** *In re City of Dickinson*, 568 S.W.3d 642, 646 (Tex. 2019) (Devine, J.) holds that Rule 192.3(a)'s "not privileged" limit governs Rule 192.3(e)(6) absent a specific provision otherwise — the structural move the `WS-3` entry makes about 192.3(h), from the Texas Supreme Court, about a sibling subsection. Adding it gives the derivation note a Supreme Court source for its method while leaving its honest core intact (no located case says "192.3(a) governs 192.3(h)" in those terms). **Cost first: `WS-3` is VERIFIED as of #110, and any wording change DETACHES that verification and needs a later re-verification look.** Adopt, reject, or edit. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`Q-WS3P-2`** — **Do you adopt `C-DICK-2`?** The same opinion at 648: *"Unlike work product, which is expressly discoverable under Rule 192.5(c)(1), the rules do not mention the discovery of attorney-client communications."* It supports the entry's SCOPE boundary — the same boundary the entry draws and the same rule its cross-reference points to. **Same detachment cost.** Put separately from `Q-WS3P-1` because you may want one and not the other. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`Q-WS3P-3`** — **Do you adopt `C-JIM-1`, and does it change how the entry cites *Jimenez*?** The entry records *Jimenez* only as applying 192.5(c)(1) and ordering production. It **also considered and rejected the comment-9 shield argument**, in terms tied to its posture: *"In circumstances like these, when the only privilege asserted is 'work product' and when rule 192.5(c) plainly states…"* — whether those clauses **limit** the holding or merely describe the facts is a reading, not the court's declaration, and the candidate says so on its face. Adopting narrows the risk that *Jimenez* is later read as contrary authority **and** cuts against over-reading `C-ARD-1` as a categorical "statement to carrier is protected" rule. **Same detachment cost. And the cite consequence: the entry names *Jimenez* in prose with NO citation at all, so this supplies a full citation — a cite act, its own act under ROUTE-C, needing its own word from you.** — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`Q-WS3P-4`** — **Does `Q-WS3-5` now close, or does the second pass reopen it?** #108 closed it as a question and left the read as a pending act. **The act has run and the answer is "no."** But it produced three candidates, none adopted, so there is a live successor. Does that ride as a new row, or as an amendment to the existing one? A session should not pick this. — **OPEN — Michael**
- ⬜ **`Q-WS3P-5`** — **Do you want *In re Baytown Nissan*, 451 S.W.3d 140 (Bland, J.), read for the `C-XL-1` "representative of the client" question rather than for comment 9?** It concerns privilege over a lawyer-to-lawyer conversation across a trade-association-and-member line, conditionally granting relief as to the member's counsel and denying it as to the association's. **That was not the authorized question, its TRE 503 passages were not read in full, and this packet therefore does not characterize it for that purpose.** Reading it for it is a separate authorization. — **OPEN — Michael**

State*'s ninth vote resolved?** Johnson, J. is absent from the eight-Justice joining list, and the retrieval positively established a sibling **concurrence by Johnson, J.** ("I concur in the judgment of this Court, but not its reasoning"), which **was not read.** Almost certainly immaterial — *Pope* is a CCA criminal work-product case with no bearing on TRCP 192.3(h) — and recorded only so the limit is disclosed rather than discovered later. — **OPEN — Michael**

- ✅ **V-9 — NEW 2026-08-13 (#73), opened by the V-8 ruling. What does the majority-opinion rule
  require when the data cannot support positive identification?** CLAUDE.md's majority-opinion rule
  is BINDING and prescribes a method: enumerate `sub_opinions`, then identify the majority
  **positively** by opinion-type marker and authoring language, never by position or assumption.
  ***Allstate Ins. Co. v. Irwin* is a case where that method cannot run at all** — two clusters
  (4885466, 4885465), each a single untyped `010combined` with no author, no per-curiam marker, and
  an empty citations array; a probe of the 26,366-character stored text for "delivered the opinion
  of the Court" returned **zero matches**, so even the authoring-language fallback does not fire.
  **The question: what does the rule require in that state — refuse to characterize the opinion at
  all, fall back to the reporter, or something else — and should that fallback be written into
  CLAUDE.md's rule 5?** Raised because the rule currently has no stated answer, and silence in a
  binding rule gets filled by whoever hits it next. **Michael's act; nothing edited.** Source:
  `registry-citator-pass-2026-08-13.md` §4. *Alford
  Chevrolet-Geo* has two FLP clusters on one reporter cite (2419858, filed 1999-08-26, and 5269700
  "In re Chevroletgeo", filed 1999-06-10, same docket 97-1171); *Irwin* has two clusters with no
  reporter cite at all and no opinion typing, so the majority-opinion rule's method has nothing to
  work from; and citing counts disagree across three FLP surfaces (Alford: 187 search index / 207
  clusters API / 168 `cites:` query, same case, same session). All are retrieval hazards of the
  same family as the majority-opinion rule, and all were found in a sample of ten cases. **The
  question: should `registry-courtlistener-integration-design.md` §0.1 record a duplicate-cluster
  hazard and a "citing counts are not precise" rule alongside the existing cluster-vs-sub-opinion

  either way. Source: workbook §5 Q5 and `registry-citator-pass-2026-08-13.md` §1, §2.3, §4.

Entry `4a` lists "LaPorte v. State, 840 S.W.2d 412" as a candidate **with no pinpoint.** **Do you want the candidate line pinpointed to 414 and annotated that 415 is overruled?** *(This is a change to a registry entry's candidate-authority line and is therefore ROUTE-C-adjacent — flagged as its own act, never done silently.)* Source: doc 5 §7. **AMENDED 2026-08-18 (#103) — THE REMEDY WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED; THE QUESTION SURVIVES IN A DIFFERENT FORM.** ***LaPorte does not split by page.*** Both halves are on **415**, in adjacent paragraphs — the `[*415]` page marker precedes the "single criminal action" holding sentence, and the void-sentence language follows it; *Ex parte Carter* n.4 pin-cites the definition **"412, 414-15."** **So a pinpoint CANNOT separate the good half from the overruled half, and the original instruction "Never cite 415" would have barred counsel from the very sentence stating the rule.** Two further corrections ride with it: *Carter*'s reaffirmation is a **FOUR-vote plurality and dictum** (the case was decided on cognizability) against **five** votes for the overruling; and **"may be raised at any time" was NARROWED, not overruled** — it does not control cognizability on habeas, while on direct appeal § 3.03 remains a Marin waiver-only right (*Stiger*, 2024, citing LaPorte at 415 for it). **THE CORRECTED REMEDY: a parenthetical — *"overruled on other grounds by Ex parte Carter"* — not a pinpoint. - ⬜ **`Q-AUTH-2`** — **NEW 2026-08-18 (#104). A precedential-value question the copy that was read cannot answer.** ***Whaley v. State*** (Dallas 2020) is a **criminal** court-of-appeals opinion, so **TRAP 47.2(b)** requires it to bear “publish” or “do not publish” — and **that notation could not be found anywhere in the copy read.** FLP reports `status: “Published”`, but per `#103` **FLP’s status field is not evidence of a Texas designation.** Under **TRAP 47.7(a)**, if *Whaley* is not designated for publication it has **no precedential value** and may be cited only with the notation — so its weight is UNRESOLVED and it should not be relied on until the notation is read off the court’s own document. **Do you want the court’s own document pulled so the notation can be read, or is *Whaley* dropped as unresolved?** *(Not needed if *Hurlburt* is adopted — see `Q-RL6-1`.)* Source: `costs-and-privilege-authority-read-2026-08-18.md` §2, §3.4, §5. — **OPEN — your hand** **⚠ ANNOTATED ADD-ONLY 2026-08-18, RUNNER-FOUND AT THE FIFTY-EIGHTH INVOCATION — THE PREMISE OF THIS QUESTION IS WRONG, AND THE ANSWER IS ADVERSE. R. APP. P. 47.2 (b) — 181255F.U05”* (read at `opinion_id` 4494303, character position 11086; the trailing `U` in the court’s own file stamp `181255F.U05` is its unpublished marker). **So *Whaley* is DO NOT PUBLISH, and under TRAP 47.7(a) it has no precedential value and may be cited only with the notation.** **Two consequences you should have in front of you.** (1) **No document needs pulling** — the act this row proposes is already done, and the answer is the unfavourable one, so the live question is narrowed to whether *Whaley* is dropped outright or retained with the notation. (2) **This is a concrete, live proof of `#103`’s own rule that FLP’s status field is not evidence of a Texas designation** — FLP reports `status: “Published”` for this very opinion, and the court’s own document says the opposite. **Nothing was closed and no packet content was edited: the read document and `#104` land verbatim, per the `Q-T19-3` precedent. This annotation is the runner’s act, not the packet’s.** — **STILL OPEN — your call, on corrected facts**
